**2. Library vs. hand-rolled?**
- **react-i18next** (industry standard): ~12–15 kB gzip → **blows the 243.5–246.5 kB bundle band**, requires a deliberate D24 band reset + the regression test update (allowed under your rules since it's an intentional behaviour change).
- **Hand-rolled** (H10-friendly): a typed `t()` over static JSON dictionaries + React context, ~1–2 kB. No plurals/interpolation engine unless we need it. Likely sufficient for a portal with mostly labels/headings.

**3. Scope of translation?**
- UI chrome only (nav, buttons, headers, validation messages)?
- Or also data-adjacent formatting — dates (DD/MM/YYYY display), numbers, CSV export headers, status badge labels?

**4. Language selection & persistence?**
Proposed default: a switcher in the header, persisted in `localStorage` under its own key (NOT the session envelope — avoids H7 v2 bump), browser-language default on first visit.

**5. Who translates?**
Do you have Urdu translations available, or do we ship English + keys scaffolded so translations drop in later?

**My recommended defaults** (answer "go with defaults" if these fit): English + Urdu incl. RTL · hand-rolled `t()` per H10 · UI chrome + validation messages, dates stay DD/MM/YYYY · header switcher + localStorage · scaffold keys now, translations drop in later.

Once you confirm, I'll spawn **project-architect** for a short ADR (ADR-0005) + key-structure plan, then **frontend-developer** for implementation after you approve the plan. QA remains waived — gate-verified only.

## [45] 👤 User — 14:13

OWNER LIVE-REVIEW AMENDMENTS — DONE, one cycle, both folded.

Files changed (3, app code):
- src/i18n/index.ts — added syncHtmlLang(lang) helper + i18n.on("languageChanged", syncHtmlLang) listener; also synced once at module load. Keeps <html lang> in sync with active language.
- src/styles/theme.css — added one CSS block inside @layer base scoping unicode-bidi: plaintext to text-bearing elements when html[lang="ur"]. Makes each inline text run pick its own direction from content (Urdu → RTL, English/digits → LTR). Layout containers (flex, grid, table columns, sidebar order) NOT touched — unicode-bidi operates at bidi-embedding level, not layout. Mixed phone/CNIC visually stay LTR because digits are strong-LTR.
- src/components/LanguageSwitcher.tsx — variant=secondary → variant=outline plus className border-primary/40 bg-background text-foreground hover:bg-accent hover:text-accent-foreground. Brand-tinted subtle border makes button clearly delineated on all 3 mounts (sidebar header, dashboard header, auth shell). Existing className prop preserved and appended.

Judgement calls:
1. Mechanism for text RTL — chose CSS unicode-bidi: plaintext on a targeted selector set (body, h1-h4, p, label, span, li, td, th, button, a, input, textarea) rather than stamping dir="auto" on every JSX element. Simpler (one CSS rule vs touching hundreds of components — H10), can't accidentally flip a layout container, and plaintext defers to first-strong-character per inline which is exactly what mixed Urdu+phone/CNIC needs. direction property NOT set — that would flip layout on inheritance. Only unicode-bidi is set, so table columns / flex order / popovers stay LTR by construction.
2. Not using dir="rtl" on <html> — would cascade to layout containers and flip sidebar/tables/popovers, forbidden by ADR-0005 R1. Kept out.
3. html[lang="ur"] selector — driven off lang attr (synced by i18n listener) rather than JS class toggle — keeps first-paint consistent (attribute set at i18n module init before React mounts) and no re-render on language change.
4. Switcher border color — border-primary/40 (brand green at 40% opacity). Visible on both white sidebar bg and auth screen bg; uses design token, no magic hex.
5. No locale VALUE edits — en.json/ur.json byte-identical to prior cycle.
6. No new tests — per constraint.

Gates — all 7 fresh, all green:
- lint: 0 issues
- typecheck (tsc --noEmit): 0 errors
- typecheck:strict (tsc --noEmit --strict): 0 errors
- build (vite build, fresh dist): 39.21s, dist/assets/index-*.js gzip 273.29 kB (Vite CLI). +0.02 kB from prior cycle (was 273.27).
- rg msw dist/assets/*.js: 0 hits
- bundle-size guard (vitest zlib): 266.89 kB — INSIDE band 265.4–268.4. +0.02 kB from prior 266.87. Constants NOT rewritten.
- npm test: 115 passed / 115 (16 files) in 32.84s

Band note: 266.89 sits mid-band, well within ±1.5. No reset triggered.

One-line commit message (plain, no prefixes/jargon):
Make Urdu text flow right-to-left without moving any layout, and make the language button visible with a subtle border

Open questions:
1. RTL scope — text-only (via unicode-bidi: plaintext) sufficient at live review, or does owner want stronger visual cue like text-align: start on headings so they anchor to the right edge in Urdu? I intentionally did NOT add text-align because that shifts alignment of every heading/label — flirts with 'no layout mirror' constraint.

Judgement calls:
1. text-align: right rather than text-align: start — because direction: rtl is intentionally NOT set on these elements (would risk cascading to any flex/grid children if a heading later gets any). With direction still default LTR, start = left. Explicit right is the safe way to anchor to the right edge without setting direction.
2. td left as default — noted above. If owner at review wants td to also anchor right, one-line follow-up to add td to the set.
3. Kept the two rules as two blocks (unicode-bidi block and text-align block) rather than merging — the selector sets differ (text-align set is a strict subset), and merging would either force text-align on the broader set (bad for input/textarea/button) or duplicate the entire selector list. Two blocks is simpler.

(2) Phone entry / digit typing — digits are strong-LTR characters, so a run like "03001234567" flows left-to-right as a sequence. text-align: right anchors the entire run to the right edge. Cursor behaviour: cursor sits at the end of the typed run (right edge on empty, right-of-last-digit as user types) — same UX as any RTL app's numeric field. unicode-bidi: plaintext on the input already handled the character-flow correctness; text-align: right adds the anchor.

All three use the same pattern: <Search> icon absolute left-3 (12px from left, ~28px right edge including icon width) + <Input> with pl-9 pr-3 (36px left padding for icon, 12px right padding). Urdu-anchored placeholder/typed text starts at 12px from the right edge and extends leftward. Search icon is on the LEFT — no overlap risk on the right side. Even at long query lengths, text scrolls under the left icon area only after passing the pl-9 boundary, same as English.

(4) English mode — every text-align rule is inside html[lang="ur"] scope. English users get default browser text-align (start = left for LTR). Zero cascade.

(5) DatePickerField trigger — untouched. It renders a <button>, not <input>. Not in the text-align selector set (button was already excluded to keep centered button labels intact).

Additional coverage note:
- shadcn Input primitive at src/components/ui/input.tsx is the <input> element under the hood. The rule targets bare input tag globally in Urdu scope, so it hits both FieldInput and any bare Input usage automatically. No component-level changes needed.
- Any <input type="checkbox"> or <input type="radio"> in the tree also matches, but text-align does not affect those (no text content in a checkbox/radio). Harmless.
- <input type="file"> — text-align: right on a file input shifts the "No file chosen" label to the right in Chrome/Firefox. Non-issue for this app since no file inputs are currently used. Flag if that ever changes.

Ah, I see the issue now — the `flex` utility class is beating the base layer rule. In Tailwind's cascade, utilities come after base, so `@layer base` declarations lose to utility classes like `flex`. That means my `display: block` rule in the base layer can't override the `flex` class on the Label component. The teammate's earlier observation about text-center utilities overriding base rules is the exact same problem here. I need to either use `!important` to force the override, move the rule into the utilities layer, or try a different approach like adjusting `justify-content` since the Label is a flex container. The simplest fix would be adding `!important` to the display rule or putting it in the utilities layer instead.

OWNER BUGS FIXED — labels anchor right + Nastaliq clipping resolved. Both verified visually in a real browser (Playwright, MSW on, admin login, screenshots + computed styles inspected).

ROOT CAUSE (label bug, per your diagnosis — confirmed)
- My previous @layer base display:block rule lost to Tailwind utility layer. shadcn Label carries a flex utility that beat the base cascade.
- Fixed: replaced with html[lang="ur"] label { justify-content: flex-end; }. No competing utility sets justify-content on Label so the base rule now wins cleanly (no !important needed). display:block rule REMOVED.

NASTALIQ CLIPPING FIX (Urdu-scoped, CSS-only, 1 file: src/styles/theme.css)
1. line-height: 2 !important on body/p/li/td/th/label/legend/input/textarea/button/a/[role=tab]/[role=menuitem]/sidebar-menu-button/sidebar-menu-sub-button/badge. !important required — Tailwind utilities (text-xs with implicit leading, leading-*, etc.) win base cascade otherwise.
2. line-height: 1.8 !important on h1-h4.
3. Fixed-height → height:auto + min-height (h-7..h-12): so containers grow to fit Nastaliq without shrinking below English minimums. Uses calc(var(--spacing) * n) to match Tailwind's spacing scale exactly.
4. overflow: visible !important on sidebar-menu-button, sidebar-menu-sub-button, tabs-list, tabs-trigger, badge (Tailwind overflow-hidden utility on shadcn primitives clipped Nastaliq bottom hooks).
5. sidebar-menu-button/sub-button descendant span: overflow:visible + text-overflow:clip + white-space:normal + line-height:2 !important — this was the actual clip point owner saw in the screenshot (inner truncate span, not the button).
No font-size trim — Nastaliq at current size (14-16px) reads fine with line-height:2; adding a reduction would shrink already-tight Login/nav text unnecessarily.

## [135] 👤 User — 20:31

to deploy this i need to upload dist folder right?

---

## [136] 🤖 Assistant — 20:31

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking</summary>

The owner needs to upload the `dist` folder from a Vite static build, making sure to set the API base URL environment variable to the production backend and disable MSW before building. Since this is a React Router SPA, the server needs to be configured to serve `index.html` for any unrecognized routes so client-side routing works properly.
</details>

Yes — `dist/` is the whole deployable (static SPA). But four things to get right:

1. **Build with production env first** — `VITE_API_BASE_URL` = your real API Gateway URL and `VITE_ENABLE_MSW=false` must be set **at build time** (Vite bakes them in; the current `dist/` on disk may have been built with dev values — rebuild before uploading).
2. **Upload the *contents* of `dist/`** to the host's web root (index.html at top level).
3. **SPA fallback is mandatory** — the app uses browser routing, so the server must rewrite all unknown paths to `index.html`, or refreshing/deep-linking `/admin/...` gives 404s. (S3+CloudFront: error page → `/index.html`; nginx: `try_files $uri /index.html`; most static hosts have a "SPA mode" toggle.)
4. **CORS on the backend** — calls use `credentials: 'include'`, so API Gateway must return your exact deployed origin in `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (not `*`) plus `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`, or every request will fail in production.

Also: if the maps fix used an API key, restrict it to your deployed domain in the Google console, and remember the band test is still red if you plan to run gates pre-commit.

point 3: how can i fix this?

## [138] 🤖 Assistant — 09:44

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking</summary>

I need to ask where they're deploying this since the fix varies by host—I'll suggest they tell me whether they're using S3 with CloudFront, nginx, Apache, or a platform like Amplify, Netlify, or Vercel, and I can give them the specific configuration for each.
</details>

Depends where you're hosting the `dist/`. Pick your case:

**S3 + CloudFront** (most likely for you)
- CloudFront → your distribution → **Error pages** → create two custom error responses: HTTP `403` and `404` → Response page path `/index.html` → HTTP response code `200`.
- (S3-website-endpoint only, no CloudFront: set both Index document *and* Error document to `index.html`.)

**nginx**
```nginx
location / {
  try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
}
```

**Apache** — `.htaccess` in the web root:
```apache
RewriteEngine On
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} !-f
RewriteRule ^ /index.html [L]
```

**Amplify Hosting** — Rewrites and redirects → add rule: source `</^[^.]+$|\.(?!(css|js|png|jpg|svg|json|woff2)$)([^.]+$)/>` → target `/index.html` → type `200 (Rewrite)`.

**Netlify** — file `_redirects` in `dist/`: `/* /index.html 200` · **Vercel** — auto-handled for SPAs.

**Quick test after deploy:** open `https://your-domain/admin/all-riders` directly in a fresh tab and hit refresh — if the app loads instead of a 404, you're done.

Tell me which host it is and I'll give you the exact click-path or config snippet for your setup.
